<FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">million for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 and the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 through December&nbsp;31, 2004, respectively. The Predecessor
incurred depreciation and amortization expense of $6.6 million for the period of January&nbsp;1, 2004 through September&nbsp;30, 2004. The Predecessor also incurred depreciation and amortization expense of $9.5 million for the year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2003. Repairs and maintenance costs that do not extend the useful lives of the Company&#146;s assets or enhance the utility of those assets are expensed as incurred. We were required by our franchisors to make capital expenditures
at the Hyatt Regency Rochester, Embassy Suites Hotel Phoenix-Scottsdale, the Hilton Glendale and the Embassy Suites Hotel and Casino San Juan during 2005 and are required to do so for the remainder of 2006. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Cash and Cash Equivalents</I>&#151;Cash and cash equivalents represent cash on hand and in banks and cash equivalents with maturities of less than 90
days. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Restricted Cash</I>&#151;Restricted cash consists of cash held in escrow reserves required by franchisors and/or lenders that
relate to the payment of capital expenditures, real estate taxes and insurance. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Accounts Receivable</I>&#151;Accounts receivable
consists primarily of meeting and banquet room rental and hotel guest receivables. The Company generally does not require collateral. Ongoing credit evaluations are performed and an allowance for potential credit losses is provided against the
portion of accounts receivable that is estimated to be uncollectible. There was an allowance for doubtful accounts receivable of $17 at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and $14 at December&nbsp;31, 2004. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Inventories</I>&#151;Inventories consist primarily of food and beverages, and are stated at the lower of cost or market value. Cost is generally
determined using the first-in, first-out method. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Deferred Loan Fees, Net</I>&#151;Deferred loan fees are recorded at cost and amortized
using the level yield which approximates the straight-line method over the terms of the related debt. The amount of deferred debt costs at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004 were carried at a cost of $3.1 million and $1.2 million, respectively. The
accumulated amortization was $0.4 million and $0, respectively, as of December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Investments&#151;</I>Marketable
securities are comprised primarily of mutual funds, stocks and stock funds carried at market value. In accordance with SFAS 115, the investments in marketable securities have been classified as available for sale. The recorded cost of investments
which are considered to be available for sale is adjusted to fair market value. The difference between cost and fair value is classified as accumulated other comprehensive income in the equity section of the balance sheet. The cost of the marketable
securities reflected in the accompanying consolidated and combined balance sheets at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, is $0.3 million and $3.3 million, respectively. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Advertising Costs</I>&#151;Advertising costs are expensed as incurred. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 and for the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 to December&nbsp;31, 2004, the Company incurred
advertising costs of approximately $8.4 million and $1.5 million, respectively. For the period from January&nbsp;1, 2004 to September&nbsp;30, 2004, and for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003, the Predecessor incurred advertising costs of
approximately $4.3 million and $5.3 million, respectively. Advertising costs are included in hotel expenses in the accompanying consolidated and combined statements of operations. We also pay advertising fees pursuant to the franchise agreements
with Marriott and Hilton. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Hotel Expenses</I>&#151;Hotel expenses include hotel-level general and administrative fees, sales,
advertising, and marketing expenses, repairs and maintenance expenses, franchise fees, utility costs, hotel labor costs, real estate taxes, franchise fees, costs of food and beverage, and insurance. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Deferred Franchise Asset</I>&#151;Deferred franchise asset at December&nbsp;31, 2004 represents
the fair value of the franchise agreements associated with the Marriott, Hilton, and Embassy Suites hotels. These fees are carried at a cost of $0.8 million and $0.5 million at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. The accumulated
amortization at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, respectively, was $0.1 million and $0. The cost carried by the Predecessor at December&nbsp;31, 2003 was $0.6 million with accumulated amortization of $0.2 million. These agreements have varying
remaining lives, from 2010 to 2025, and we have valued those remaining lives accordingly. The deferred franchise fees for the Predecessor consisted of the initial franchise fees paid to Marriott International, Inc. and Hilton Hotels, less the
amortization of those fees. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Income Taxes</I>&#151;As a REIT, the Company generally will not be subject to federal corporate income tax
on that portion of its net income (loss) that does not relate to taxable REIT subsidiaries. However, EHP TRS Holding Co., Inc. is treated as a taxable REIT subsidiary for federal income tax purposes. In addition, the REIT and its subsidiaries are
subject to various state and local income taxes. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Earnings (Loss) Per Share</I>&#151;Basic earnings (loss) per share is calculated by
dividing net income (loss) available to common shareholders by the weighted average common shares outstanding during the period. Dilutive earnings (loss) per share reflects the potential dilution that could occur if securities or other contracts to
issue common shares were exercised or converted into common shares, whereby such exercise or conversion would result in lower earnings (loss) per share. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Stock-Based Compensation</I>&#151;The Company accounts for stock-based compensation using the intrinsic-value method in accordance with Accounting Principles Board Opinion No.&nbsp;25, &#147;Accounting for Stock
Issued Employees.&#148; In connection with the Company&#146;s formation, the Company established the 2004 Long-Term Incentive Plan (the &#147;Stock Plan&#148;). The Company has issued a net total of 465,012&nbsp;shares of restricted stock under the
Stock Plan to its executives, directors, and certain employees of the Company, the Predecessor and its affiliates. The vesting periods for these shares range from one to five years. Such shares are charged to compensation expense on a straight-line
basis over the vesting period based on the price on the date of issuance. For the year ending December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company incurred total compensation expense of $2.1 million related to these restricted shares. For the period from inception
through December&nbsp;31, 2004, the Company recognized compensation expense of approximately $0.6 million related to these restricted shares. Under the Stock Plan, the Company may issue a variety of performance-based stock awards, including
nonqualified stock options. At December&nbsp;31, 2005, no performance-based stock awards have been issued other than the restricted stock discussed above. The amount of shares available for issuance under this plan increased by 131,435 shares on
January&nbsp;1, 2005, pursuant to the provisions of the plan. At December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company had approximately 529,755 remaining shares available for future issuance under the Stock Plan. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>2004 Long-Term Incentive Plan&#151;</I>The Company&#146;s 2004 Long-Term Incentive Plan was adopted by the board of directors and approved by
stockholders prior to the initial public offering. The purpose of the incentive plan is to promote the Company&#146;s success and enhance the value by linking the personal interests of participants to those of the stockholders, and by providing such
persons with an incentive to achieve outstanding performance. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The incentive plan authorizes the governance and compensation committee and
the Company&#146;s board of directors upon approval to grant awards to employees, officers, consultants (including, but not limited to, Corporex and its employees) and directors in the following forms: </FONT></P> <P

on the last day of our fiscal year in each year, beginning in 2004, equal to the lesser of the following: </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

SIZE="2"><I>Segments</I>&#151;The Company presently operates in one business segment within the hotel lodging industry: direct hotel investments. Direct hotel investments refers to owning hotels through either acquisition or new development. The
Predecessor also only operated within the direct hotel investments segment. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Recent Accounting Pronouncements&#151; </I></FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In January 2003, the FASB issued Interpretation No.&nbsp;46 (revised December 2003) (&#147;FIN46R&#148;), &#147;Consolidation of Variable Interest
Entities, an interpretation of Accounting research Bulletin No.&nbsp;51, Consolidated Financial Statements.&#148; FIN 46R revises the requirements for consolidation by business enterprises of variable interest entities with specific characteristics.
FIN46R is effective immediately for variable interests created after December&nbsp;31, 2003, and to all other entities no later than the beginning of the first reporting period beginning after March&nbsp;15, 2004. Additional clarification and
guidance was issued during 2004 and 2005. The Company has adopted FIN46R and in compliance with this pronouncement and for the period ended December&nbsp;31, 2004, consolidated the Embassy Suites Hotel Cincinnati- RiverCenter into the financial
statements as a result of our 49% ownership in this hotel and the Company was required to acquire the remaining 51% interest in the hotel in exchange for 427,485 partnership units no later than January&nbsp;31, 2006. The Company exercised this right
and acquired the remaining 51% interest in this hotel on December&nbsp;5, 2005. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In December 2004, the FASB issued Statement 123 (revised
2004) which establishes standards for the accounting for transactions in which an entity exchanges its equity instruments for goods or services, primarily on accounting for transactions in which an entity obtains employee services in share-based
transactions. The FASB and SEC issued additional guidance and clarification to this Statement throughout 2005. This Statement requires a public entity to measure the cost of employee services received in exchange for an award of equity instruments
based on the grant-date fair value of the award, with limited exceptions. That cost will be recognized over the period during which an employee is required to provide services in </FONT>
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Concentrations of Risk </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">All of the Company&#146;s
investments are concentrated in the hotel industry. The Company&#146;s current investment strategy is to acquire or develop full service and full service all-suites hotels. At present, all of the Company&#146;s hotels are located in the United
States. Accordingly, adverse conditions in the U.S. hotel industry will have a material adverse effect on the Company&#146;s operating and investment revenues and cash available for distribution to stockholders. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Three of the Company&#146;s hotels are located in the Northern Kentucky/Greater Cincinnati, Ohio metropolitan area. Economic and real estate conditions
in the area significantly affect our revenues. Business layoffs, downsizing, industry slowdowns, demographic changes and other similar factors may adversely affect the economic climate in this locale. Any resulting oversupply or reduced demand for
hotels in the area would adversely affect revenues and net income. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:18px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Income Taxes </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company has elected to be taxed
as a REIT under the Internal Revenue Code. To qualify as a REIT, the Company must meet a number of organizational and operational requirements, including a requirement that the Company distributes at least 90% of the Company&#146;s taxable income to
the Company&#146;s stockholders. The Company currently intends to adhere to these requirements and maintain the Company&#146;s REIT status. If the Company fails to qualify as a REIT in any taxable year, the Company will be subject to federal income
taxes at regular corporate rates (including any applicable alternative minimum tax) and may not qualify as a REIT for four subsequent taxable years. If the Company qualifies for taxation as a REIT, the Company may be subject to certain state and
local taxes on the Company&#146;s income as well as to federal income and excise taxes on the Company&#146;s undistributed taxable income. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Minority Interest </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Minority interest in the
operating partnership represents the limited partners&#146; proportionate share of the equity in the operating partnership. Net income (loss) is allocated to minority interest based on limited partnership percentage ownership for the period. Upon
formation of the Company on October&nbsp;1, 2004, the Company issued 5,566,352&nbsp;units of limited partnership interest to affiliates, plus another 427,485 partnership units were issued on December&nbsp;5, 2005 in exchange for the remaining 51% of
the Embassy Suites Hotel Cincinnati&#151;RiverCenter. An additional 250,000 partnership units were issued to the former owners of the Embassy Suites Hotel Denver International Airport on December&nbsp;5, 2005, in compliance with the earn-out
provisions in the original purchase agreement. This total of 6,243,837 of partnership units represents an approximate minority interest ownership of 26%. After a one-year holding period from the date of issuance, partnership units may be redeemed
for the cash value of one share of our common stock or, at our sole option, one share of common stock. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:18px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Commitments and Contingencies </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Restricted
Cash</I>&#151;Under certain existing loan agreements, the Company is obligated to escrow payments for insurance and real estate taxes. In addition, for provisions in management company agreements, or debt or loan agreements related to certain
properties require the Company to escrow 4% of gross revenue for capital improvements. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Contingent Consideration</I>&#151;The purchase
price excludes earn-out payments totaling in the aggregate up to 833,333 additional operating partnership units, which will be payable over a three-year period to the original contributors of the Embassy Suites Hotel Columbus/Dublin, the Embassy
Suites Hotel Cleveland/Rockside and the Embassy Suites Hotel Denver-International Airport only if certain operating measurements are achieved on any trailing 12-month basis within three years from the contribution. As the Embassy Suites Hotel
Denver-International Airport met its earn-out requirements for the 12-month period ending September&nbsp;30, 2005, an additional 250,000 operating partnership units were issued to the former owners of this hotel on December&nbsp;5, 2005. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Franchise Fees</I>&#151;All of our hotels, except the Hyatt Regency Rochester, operate under franchise agreements. In conjunction with these
franchise agreements the Company is obligated to pay the franchisors royalty fees between 4% and 6% of gross room revenue, and fees for marketing, reservations, and other related activities aggregating between 1% and 4% of gross room revenue. In
addition, the Marriott hotels are obligated to pay between 2% and 3% for food and beverage revenues. Franchise fees are included in hotel expenses in the accompanying consolidated and combined statements of operations. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">These franchise agreements expire beginning in 2010 through 2025. When the term of a franchise expires, the franchisor has no obligation to issue a new
franchise. The loss of a franchise could have a material adverse effect on the operations or the underlying value of the affected hotel because of the loss of associated name recognition, marketing support, and centralized reservation systems
provided by the franchisor. The loss of a franchise could also have a material adverse effect on cash available for distribution to stockholders. In addition, if the Company terminates a franchise prior to its expiration date, the Company would be
required to pay a termination fee. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Hyatt Regency Rochester Management Agreement</I>&#151;The Hyatt Regency Rochester is managed under
an agreement with the Hyatt Corporation that expires in 2022. Under the terms of this agreement the Hyatt Corporation receives a base management fee equal to 4% of the gross revenue of the hotel. Hyatt Corporation is also eligible to receive an
incentive fee, payable in monthly installments, equal to the amount by which 20% of the profit for a fiscal year exceeds the base fee for that fiscal year. The Company paid incentive fees to Hyatt of $61 and $0 for the year ended December&nbsp;31,
2005 and the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 to December&nbsp;31, 2004, respectively. The Predecessor paid incentive fees to Hyatt of $32 and $20 for the period from January&nbsp;1, 2004 to September&nbsp;30, 2004 and for the year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2003, respectively. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

period. The rent year commences June of each year. The Company can elect to convert this rent year to a calendar year whereby the $4.5 million would be prorated for the first year calculation. The Company has not made this calendar year election.
The &#147;air rights&#148; lease agreement expires in June 2015 and has five consecutive automatic renewal options of 25 years each unless the Company gives written notice to the City that it is not renewing the lease. The Company incurred rent
expense (in thousands) of approximately $0.2 million and $0.1 million for the period ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 and from October&nbsp;1, 2004 to December&nbsp;31, 2004, respectively. Rent expense incurred by the Predecessor for the periods ended
September&nbsp;30, 2004 and December&nbsp;31, 2003 (in thousands) was approximately $0.2 million for each period. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Cincinnati Landmark

is based on a spread over LIBOR of 160 basis points to 325 basis points, depending on the Company&#146;s leverage levels.&nbsp;Based on the Company&#146;s current leverage, the current rate is LIBOR plus 225 basis points. Proceeds from this
permanent credit facility were used, in part, to pay off the 90-day bridge facility the Company entered into with U.S. Bank on October&nbsp;3, 2005. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, Eagle granted approximately (in thousands) 45 shares of restricted stock (weighted average grant price of $9.51 per share), valued at approximately $0.4 million, under its
LTIP and cancelled approximately 22 shares of restricted stock (weighted average grant price of $9.75 per share) due to the employee resignations, valued at $0.2 million. For the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 through December&nbsp;31, 2004, the
Company issued approximately (in thousands) 441 shares of restricted stock, valued at $4.3 million. There were no cancellations during this period. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman"
SIZE="2">For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 and the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 to December&nbsp;31, 2004, interest paid by the Company was approximately $10.2 million (net of $0.2 million of capitalized interest) and $3.3 million,
respectively. For the period from January&nbsp;1, 2004 to September&nbsp;30, 2004, and for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003, interest paid by the Predecessor was approximately $7.6&nbsp;million and $13.3&nbsp;million, respectively. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">During the period ended September&nbsp;30, 2004, EHP Burr Ridge, LLC, the entity that owns the Marriott Burr Ridge hotel, was deemed to have been
contributed to the Predecessor. The Predecessor assumed the $22.8 million basis in the hotel, $12.2 million in debt and equity of $11.3 million. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman"
SIZE="2">Total dividends paid to holders of common stock and operating partnership unites during the period ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 were $16.3 million. Total dividends paid to holders preferred stock during the period ended December&nbsp;31,
2005 were $4.5 million. There were no dividends paid by the Company for the period from October&nbsp;1, 2004 through December&nbsp;31, 2004. The Predecessor paid distributions of $2.9 million and $4.8 million, respectively, for the periods ending
September&nbsp;30, 2004 and December&nbsp;31, 2003. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:18px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>EAGLE HOSPITALITY PROPERTIES TRUST, INC. AND PREDECESSOR </B></FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED AND COMBINED FINANCIAL STATEMENT&#151;(Continued) </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Restricted cash in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet consists primarily of escrow accounts
required by the lenders and franchisors. The escrow reserves relate to the payment of capital expenditures and real estate taxes. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Certain
debt agreements have covenants that include maintaining a required debt service coverage ratio, maintaining furniture, fixtures and equipment reserves and levels of unrestricted liquidity by owners. Eagle was in compliance with all of these
covenants as of December&nbsp;31, 2005. As long as we are in compliance with these covenants, and the distributions are permitted by state law, there are no restrictions on the Eagle&#146;s ability to make distributions to its stockholders.
</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Cincinnati Landmark Marriott in August 2001 entered into an interest rate swap agreement (&#147;Swap&#148;) fixing the 2001
financing&#146;s interest rate at 4.8%. The Swap matured in August 2004. The fair value of the Swap at December&nbsp;31, 2003 was ($716) and is recorded as a component of accumulated other comprehensive income (loss). The notional principal amount
related to the Swap was $29.7 million at December&nbsp;31, 2003. The Swap was designated a fair value hedge under SFAS 133 &#147;Accounting for Derivative Instruments and Hedging Activities.&#148; </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="font-size:18px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

matching funds vest immediately in accordance with the &#147;safe harbor&#148; provisions. Contributions to the plan for the period ending December&nbsp;31, 2005 were $14. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Predecessor participated in a 401(k) plan for qualified employees. The Predecessor matched 50% of employee contributions up to a total match of 6% of

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Comprehensive Income </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The purpose of reporting
comprehensive income is to report a measure of all changes in equity of an enterprise that result from recognized transactions and other economic events of the period other than transactions with owners in their capacity as owners. Other
comprehensive income results from adjustments due to the fluctuation of the value of interest rate swaps accounted for under SFAS No.&nbsp;133, as amended, and due to changes in the unrealized gains (losses) on marketable securities classified as
available for sale pursuant to SFAS No.&nbsp;115. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The adjustment of the unrealized (losses) gains on securities is as follows; </FONT></P>
